⟦q29.1|1+2+3⟧ Rufinus, who wrote the Ecclesiastical History, recorded something like this done in olden times by children at play. ⟦q29.1|1+2+3⟧ For he speaks of holy Athanasius — the great champion and herald of the truth, who presided over the great city of the Alexandrians and shepherded all soberly and as was pleasing to God — telling of his boyhood, and how his advance to the presidency came to him from God and took its beginning by divine foreknowledge; [?] to take up the man's early history, and to recall what manner of life he led from a boy, and what upbringing, as we have received it; and for us too to bring it before the hearer.

⟦q29.1|1+2+3⟧ Alexander, who is among the saints, pope of Alexandria, who came after Achillas according to the prophecy of holy Peter, archbishop and martyr, the one who condemned the impious Arius — one day, as he was looking out and watching the sea, he sees some playing by the shore, as is the custom of children, and imitating a bishop and all that is customarily done in the church. ⟦q29.1|4⟧ And watching a long while, he saw that some of the hidden things of the mysteries were being done by them. ⟦q29.1|5⟧ So he was troubled, and at once called the clerics and showed them what they were doing, and ordered them to go and seize all the children and bring them to him. ⟦q29.1|6⟧ And when they came he asked what their game was and what they had been doing. ⟦q29.1|7⟧ And they were frightened, being children, and tell him the whole thing duly: that they had even baptized some catechumens through Athanasius, whom those children had set up for themselves as a bishop. ⟦q29.1|8⟧ Then Alexander inquired of them carefully whom they had baptized; and having learned, and found that they had performed all those things according to the custom of our religion, he took counsel with his own clerics and ruled that those who had been granted the holy washing ought not to be baptized a second time. ⟦q29.1|8⟧ And Athanasius and the others who had been enrolled as clerics he charged their parents to bring up in the admonition and discipline of the Lord, and Athanasius above all, whom not long after he consecrated to God. ⟦q29.1|9⟧ Enriched with divine gifts, and advanced by the high priest of that time to a higher rank, he became eminent. ⟦q29.1|10⟧ Athanasius, then, and the others whom in that game he had seemed to have as [3085] [bid:c3085a]priests or deacons, he called their parents and, under God's witness, handed them over to the Church, so that they should be brought up there.

⟦q29.1|11⟧ A little time having passed, when Athanasius had been taught thoroughly by the shorthand-writer and sufficiently by the grammarian, at once he is handed over by his parents to the priest as a faithful deposit of God, and like another Samuel he was brought up in the temple of God. ⟦q29.1|12⟧ And when Alexander in his old age went to the bishops, he was chosen to follow him and carry the vestment of the priesthood, which in the Hebrew tongue was called Ephod.

⟦q29.1|13⟧ So great were the contests of this Athanasius in the Church against the heretics that the saying might seem to have been written of him: "I will show him how many things he must suffer for my name." ⟦q29.1|14⟧ For against his persecution the whole world was banded together, and the kings of the earth were stirred, and kingdoms and armies came together against him; but he kept the oracle of God which says: "If then camps stand against me, my heart shall not fear; if a council rise up against me, in this will I hope." ⟦q29.1|15⟧ But since such and so many deeds of his are told that the greatness of the things allows me to pass over nothing, while the multitude of the deeds compels me to keep silent on much, my mind labours in uncertainty, unable to choose what I must keep and what I must leave out. ⟦q29.1|16⟧ And so let us recall a few of those that belong to the matter; the rest report will tell. ⟦q29.1|17⟧ Yet report will certainly tell less than the truth; for it can neither add anything to the truth, nor will it find anything to add.

⟦q29.2|1⟧ CHAPTER 198

⟦q29.2|1⟧ Holy Athanasius, the pope of Alexandria, was once asked whether one can be baptized while not believing according to the faith and preaching of the Christians; and if someone is baptized for some other pretexts, pretending to believe, what then will become of him, or how God receives him. ⟦q29.2|2+3⟧ And he answered, saying: "You have heard [?] from the ancients how, to the blessed Peter, the martyr, when there was a great dying, and many were simply running to baptism for fear of death, someone appeared in the form of an angel, saying: 'How long will you send up here purses that are sealed, but altogether empty, with nothing inside?' So, as far as one may guess from the angel's voice, they have the seal of baptism, since they were baptized only [3088] [bid:c3088a]thinking they would gain some benefit if they received baptism."

⟦q29.2|2+3⟧ CHAPTER 199

⟦q29.3|1⟧ One of the fathers told that a certain elder, being pure and holy, used to see angels standing at his right and at his left when he was making the oblation. ⟦q29.3|2⟧ Now he had received the oblation from heretics, and as one unskilled in the divine doctrines, when he offered he said it in simplicity and guilelessness, not knowing that he was in error.

⟦q29.3|3⟧ By a dispensation a brother came to visit him who had experience of the divine doctrines. ⟦q29.3|4⟧ And it happened that the elder made the offering in his presence; and the brother (for he was a deacon) said: "These things that thou didst say at the oblation, Father, are not of the Orthodox faith but of false belief." ⟦q29.3|5⟧ But the elder, since he saw angels when he offered, paid no heed to what was said, but despised it. ⟦q29.3|6⟧ The deacon persisted, saying: "Thou art in error, good elder; for the Church does not accept these things." ⟦q29.3|7⟧ So when the elder saw himself thus accused and reproved by the deacon, he saw the angels as usual, and asked them: "Since the deacon says this to me, what is it that he says [?]?" ⟦q29.3|8⟧ And they said to him: "Bear with him; he speaks rightly to thee." ⟦q29.3|9⟧ The elder said to them: "And why did you not tell me?" ⟦q29.3|10⟧ And they said to him: "Because God so disposed it, that man should be corrected through men." ⟦q29.3|11⟧ And from then on he corrected himself, giving thanks to God and to the brother.

⟦q29.3|11⟧ CHAPTER 200

⟦q29.4|1⟧ One of the fathers told us that a certain gifted young man went off and was apprenticed to a goldsmith, and learned the craft thoroughly. ⟦q29.4|2⟧ Then one of the patricians commissions him to make a jewelled cross, so that he might offer it to the church; and since the young man was very gifted, the master gives this work over to him. ⟦q29.4|3⟧ So the young man reckons: "This man offers so much money to Christ; why should I not also mix my wages into the cross, so that Christ may reckon me like the two mites of that widow?" ⟦q29.4|4⟧ So he reckons how much pay he is to receive, and borrows it, and puts it into the cross-work. ⟦q29.4|5⟧ Then the patrician comes and weighs the cross before the stones were set, and finds it over the weight he had given. ⟦q29.4|6⟧ He began to threaten, saying he had committed a fraud by debasing the gold. ⟦q29.4|7⟧ Then the young man says to him: "He who alone knows the secrets of the heart knows that I have done no such thing. ⟦q29.4|8⟧ But seeing how much money thou didst offer to Christ, I reckoned to put in my wages, so that I too might have a share with thee, and Christ might accept me like the two mites of that widow." ⟦q29.4|9⟧ And he, astonished at this, said: "Indeed, child? ⟦q29.4|9⟧ Didst thou reckon so?" ⟦q29.4|10+11+12⟧ He says to him: [3089] "Yes." ⟦q29.4|10+11+12⟧ He said to him: "Since thou hast reckoned this, and hast [?] made thy whole purpose common with Christ, wishing to have a share with me, behold, from this day I will make thee my son and heir." ⟦q29.4|10+11+12⟧ And he took him with him, and made him his heir.

⟦q29.5|1⟧ CHAPTER 201

⟦q29.5|1⟧ One of the fathers told: "Once I went up to Constantinople on some business. ⟦q29.5|2⟧ And as I was sitting in the church, there comes in one of the great ones of the world, very Christ-loving, and seeing me he sat down; and he began to ask about the salvation of the soul. ⟦q29.5|3+4⟧ So when I told him that to those who manage earthly things well, heavenly things are given, he said: 'Thou hast said well, Father: blessed is he who has his hope toward God and gives himself up wholly to God.' And he said: 'I am the child of one of the very illustrious of the world. ⟦q29.5|5⟧ My father was very merciful, and gave much to the poor. ⟦q29.5|6⟧ So one day he calls me and shows me all his money and says to me: "Child, which wouldst thou rather? ⟦q29.5|6⟧ Shall I leave thee the money, or Christ as thy guardian?" ⟦q29.5|7⟧ And I, edified by what he was doing, said to him: "Christ rather; for all these things are here today and pass away tomorrow." ⟦q29.5|8⟧ And from the time he heard this from me, he gave unsparingly, so that when he died he left little. ⟦q29.5|9⟧ And I, being poor, went about humbly, having my hope in God, to whom he had left me.

⟦q29.5|10⟧ 'Now there was again a certain rich man, one of the great, [bid:c3089c]who had a Christ-loving wife who feared God; and he had an only daughter. ⟦q29.5|11⟧ So the wife says to her husband: "This is the only daughter we have, and the Lord has given us so many good things; what then does she need? ⟦q29.5|12⟧ If we seek to give her to someone of our own rank who is not good in his ways, he will always afflict her; let us rather seek for her a humble man who fears the Lord, so that he may love her and cherish her according to God." ⟦q29.5|13⟧ He says to her: "Thou hast counselled well; go then to the church and pray earnestly, and sit there, and whoever comes in first, he is the one sent by the Lord." ⟦q29.5|14⟧ So she did so, and after she had prayed and sat down, I was found to be coming in at once. ⟦q29.5|14⟧ So she immediately sends a servant and calls me, and began to ask me: "Where art thou from?" ⟦q29.5|15⟧ And I said to her: "I am of this city, and the son of such a one." ⟦q29.5|16⟧ And she said: "Of that merciful man?" ⟦q29.5|17⟧ And she says to me: "Hast thou a wife?" ⟦q29.5|18⟧ I said to her: "No." ⟦q29.5|19⟧ And I told her what my father had said to me and what I had said to him. ⟦q29.5|20⟧ And glorifying the Lord she said: "Behold, the good guardian whom thou didst choose has sent thee a wife and money, that thou mayest use both in the fear of God." ⟦q29.5|21⟧ And I pray to walk my father's road to the end.'"

⟦q30.1|1⟧ [3092] CHAPTER 202

⟦q30.1|1⟧ One of the Fathers told us that a certain layman had a son who was devout, a virgin, and self-controlled in all things; indeed from a little boy he had drunk no wine. ⟦q30.1|2⟧ And he had it in mind to withdraw. ⟦q30.1|2⟧ But his father wanted to put him into business, and he would not bear it. ⟦q30.1|3⟧ He had other brothers too, but he was the eldest. ⟦q30.1|3⟧ And since his aim did not suit his father's aim, his father was always reproaching him, rebuking him for his self-control and saying, "Why art thou not like thy brother, and why dost thou not give thyself to business?" ⟦q30.1|4⟧ But he held out in silence. ⟦q30.1|5⟧ And everyone loved him for his devoutness and his chastity.

⟦q30.1|6⟧ When his father was about to die, some of his kin, and others who loved him, thinking that the father hated him, since he was always rebuking him, gathered together and said, "The father may deprive the servant of God of his inheritance. ⟦q30.1|6⟧ Let us plead for him" (for he was rich). ⟦q30.1|7⟧ And they go up to him and say to him, "We have something to ask of thee." ⟦q30.1|8⟧ He says to them, "What do you want to ask of me?" ⟦q30.1|9⟧ They say to him, "About master Abibus" (for that was his name), "that thou wilt not pass him over." ⟦q30.1|10⟧ He says to them, "Is it for him you are pleading with me?" ⟦q30.1|11⟧ They say to him, "Yes." ⟦q30.1|12⟧ He says to them, "Call him here to me." ⟦q30.1|13⟧ They thought that he wanted to rebuke him, as his custom was. ⟦q30.1|14⟧ And when he came in, he says to him, "Come near me." ⟦q30.1|15⟧ And as he came near, the father threw himself at his feet, weeping and saying, "Forgive me, my child, and pray God not to reckon it against me if I afflicted thee in anything; for thou wast seeking Christ, and I was moved by the world." ⟦q30.1|16+17+18⟧ He calls his other sons too, and says to them, "This is your master and father. ⟦q30.1|16+17+18⟧ Whatever he says: if he says, 'You have something,' you have it; if he says, 'You have nothing,' you have nothing." ⟦q30.1|16+17+18⟧ And they all wondered. ⟦q30.1|19⟧ And as soon as their father had died, he gives his brothers their share, and takes his own share and gives it all to the poor, keeping nothing back for himself. ⟦q30.1|20⟧ He built a small cell to withdraw to, and after finishing the cell he falls sick. ⟦q30.1|21⟧ So his end comes upon him. ⟦q30.1|22⟧ And his brother sat by him. ⟦q30.1|23⟧ And he says to him, "Go and give thy household some comfort, for it is a holy day." ⟦q30.1|24⟧ For it was the feast of the holy apostles. ⟦q30.1|25⟧ His brother says to him, "How shall I leave thee and go away?" ⟦q30.1|26⟧ He says to him, "Go away, and when the hour comes, I will call thee." ⟦q30.1|27⟧ So when the hour came, he rose and went to the window and knocks, and his brother looks, and he beckons to him: "Come." ⟦q30.1|28⟧ And as soon as his brother came, he gave up his spirit to the Lord. ⟦q30.1|29⟧ And they all wondered and glorified God, saying, "He has found an end worthy of the love with which he loved Christ."

⟦q30.2|1⟧ [3093] CHAPTER 203

⟦q30.2|1⟧ [bid:c3093a]One of the Fathers told us that once a certain stone-worker, of the kind they call a cabidarius, having precious stones and pearls, went aboard a ship with his servants, wishing to go abroad and trade. ⟦q30.2|2⟧ It happened, by a dispensation, that he grew fond of a certain boy of the ship, who waited on him; and the boy too was refreshed by him, eating of what he ate. ⟦q30.2|3⟧ One day the boy hears the sailors whispering and settling it among themselves to throw the cabidarius into the sea, because of the stones he had. ⟦q30.2|4⟧ Then, when the boy came in to the free man with great gloom to wait on him as usual, he says to him, "Why art thou gloomy today, lad?" ⟦q30.2|5⟧ He kept it to himself, saying nothing. ⟦q30.2|6⟧ So he asked him again, saying, "Tell me truly, what is the matter with thee?" ⟦q30.2|7⟧ Then he bursts out weeping and says to him, "The sailors have plotted so and so about thee." ⟦q30.2|8⟧ He says to him, "For certain?" ⟦q30.2|9⟧ He answered him, "Yes, that is how they have settled it about thee among themselves." ⟦q30.2|10⟧ Then he calls his servants and says to them, "Whatever I tell you, do it without hesitating and without any second thought." ⟦q30.2|11⟧ Then he spreads out a linen cloth and begins to say to them, "Bring my caskets." ⟦q30.2|12⟧ And they brought them. ⟦q30.2|13⟧ And opening them, he began to spread out the stones, and when they had all been laid out, he began to speak thus: "Is this life? ⟦q30.2|14⟧ For these I run into danger, I fight the sea, and in a little while I die, taking nothing with me out of this world?" ⟦q30.2|15⟧ He says to his servants, "Empty it all into the sea." ⟦q30.2|15⟧ So at once, at his word, they flung them into the sea. ⟦q30.2|16⟧ And the sailors were astounded, [bid:c3093c]and their plot came to nothing.

⟦q30.2|16⟧ CHAPTER 204

⟦q30.3|1⟧ Someone used to say that a man was bitten by a snake and went into a certain city to be treated. ⟦q30.3|2⟧ A devout woman who feared the Lord took him in and nursed him. ⟦q30.3|3+4+5⟧ When he had a little relief from his pains, the devil began to sow thoughts in him about her, and he began to want to touch her hand. ⟦q30.3|3+4+5⟧ But she said to him, "Not so, father; keep Christ before thee [?]. ⟦q30.3|3+4+5⟧ Remember the grief and the repentance with which thou wilt repent as thou sittest in thy cell; [3096] [bid:c3096a]remember the groans and the tears that thou wilt shed." ⟦q30.3|3+4+5⟧ When he had heard these things from her, and others like them, the warfare fled from him, and in his shame he wanted to flee, not able to look her in the face. ⟦q30.3|6⟧ But she again, in the bowels of Christ, said to him, "Do not be ashamed at all; thou still needest treatment. ⟦q30.3|6⟧ It was not of thy pure soul to think those things; it was a suggestion of the envious devil." ⟦q30.3|7⟧ And so, having nursed him without offence, she sent him away with provisions for the road.

⟦q30.4|1⟧ CHAPTER 205

⟦q30.4|1⟧ Someone told us that a brother living in a coenobium used to be sent on the coenobium's errands; and there was a devout layman in a certain village who received him in faith whenever he came to the village. ⟦q30.4|2⟧ The layman had one daughter, newly widowed, who had lived with her husband a year or two. ⟦q30.4|3⟧ So as the brother went in and out among them, he was warred against over her; but she, being sensible, perceived it, and kept herself from coming into his presence. ⟦q30.4|4⟧ One day her father went into the city nearby on some business, leaving her alone in the house.

⟦q30.4|5⟧ The brother came as usual and finds her alone in the house, and says to her, "Where is thy father?" ⟦q30.4|6⟧ She says to him, "He has gone into the city." ⟦q30.4|7⟧ So he began to be troubled by the warfare, wanting to fall upon her. ⟦q30.4|8⟧ She says to him, with good sense, "Do not be troubled at all; my father is not coming till late. ⟦q30.4|9⟧ We are here, the two of us. ⟦q30.4|9⟧ But I know that you monks do nothing without prayer. ⟦q30.4|10⟧ So get up, pray to God, and whatever he puts into thy heart, let us do it." ⟦q30.4|11⟧ But he would not bear it, but was troubled by the warfare. ⟦q30.4|12⟧ She says to him, "Hast thou really ever known a woman?" ⟦q30.4|13⟧ He says to her, "No; and that is why I want to see what it is." ⟦q30.4|14⟧ She says to him, "So that is why thou art troubled: thou dost not know the foulness of wretched women." ⟦q30.4|15⟧ And wanting to lessen his passion, she says, "I am in my monthly courses, and no one can come near me or bear the smell of me, for the stench," calling her own body wretched. ⟦q30.4|16⟧ When he had heard these things from her, and others like them, he was disgusted, and coming to himself he wept.

⟦q30.4|16⟧ So when she saw that he had come to himself, she says, "See, if I had listened to thee and been persuaded by thee, we should already be done with it, having committed the sin. ⟦q30.4|17⟧ With what face, then, couldst thou have looked upon my father after that, or gone back to thy monastery and heard the choir of those holy men singing psalms? ⟦q30.4|17⟧ So I beg thee, be sober, and do not for a little pleasure throw away all the labours [3097] [bid:c3097a]thou hast, and be robbed of eternal good things." ⟦q30.4|18⟧ Hearing these words from her, the brother, the one who suffered what this story tells, told it to the man who related it, giving thanks to God, who through her good sense and chastity had not let him fall away entirely.

⟦q30.4|18⟧ CHAPTER 206

⟦q30.5|1⟧ One of the Fathers told us that a lady of senatorial rank came to venerate the holy places; and going down to Caesarea, she preferred to stay there and keep stillness. ⟦q30.5|2⟧ So she begged the bishop, saying, "Give me a virgin, to form me and teach me the fear of God." ⟦q30.5|3⟧ So the bishop chose a humble one and gives her to her. ⟦q30.5|4⟧ After a time the bishop met her and says, "How is the virgin who was given thee?" ⟦q30.5|5⟧ She said, "She is good, but she does not profit my soul much, since she lets me do my own will, being humble. ⟦q30.5|5⟧ What I need is someone to rebuke me and not allow me to do my own will." ⟦q30.5|6⟧ So again the bishop took another, a harsh one, and gives her to her, and she would say to her, "Rich fool!" rebuking her with other such things. ⟦q30.5|7⟧ After this the bishop asked her again, "How is the virgin?" ⟦q30.5|8⟧ And she says to him, "Truly this one profits my soul." ⟦q30.5|8⟧ And so she shone out in great meekness.

⟦q30.6|1⟧ CHAPTER 207

⟦q30.6|1⟧ Abba Theonas and abba Theodore [bid:c3097c]told us that in Alexandria, in the time of Paul the patriarch, a girl was left behind by her parents, who had great wealth. ⟦q30.6|2+3+4+5+6⟧ And she was still unbaptized. ⟦q30.6|2+3+4+5+6⟧ One day, as she was going about [?] in the garden which her parents had left her (for there are gardens in the middle of the city, in the houses of the great men), while she was in the garden, then, she sees a certain man getting himself ready to hang himself. ⟦q30.6|2+3+4+5+6⟧ So she runs and says to him, "What art thou doing, good man?" ⟦q30.6|2+3+4+5+6⟧ He says to her, "Truly [?], let me be, woman, for I am in great affliction." ⟦q30.6|2+3+4+5+6⟧ The girl says to him, "Tell me the truth, and perhaps I can help thee." ⟦q30.6|7⟧ He says to her, "I owe a great deal, and I am sorely oppressed by my creditors; and I have chosen to die quickly rather than live so painful a life." ⟦q30.6|8⟧ The girl says to him, "I beg thee, see, take all I have and pay it back; only do not destroy thyself." ⟦q30.6|9⟧ And he took it and paid off the debt. ⟦q30.6|10⟧ But she was in straits after that, and having no one to care for her, bereft as she was of parents, she was forced to it, and began to play the harlot.

⟦q30.6|11⟧ And some of those who knew her, and in what standing her parents had been, said, "Who knows the judgements of God, how a soul is allowed to fall away for some cause?"

⟦q30.6|12⟧ Yet after some time she fell sick, and she came to herself. ⟦q30.6|13⟧ And pierced with compunction she said to her neighbours, "For the Lord's sake, have mercy on my soul and [3100] speak to the pope, that he make me a Christian." ⟦q30.6|14⟧ And they all despised her, saying, "Will he really receive this one, a harlot as she is?" ⟦q30.6|15⟧ And she was in great affliction because of it. ⟦q30.6|16⟧ While she was in this state, and losing heart, an angel of the Lord stood by her in the form of the man to whom she had done the mercy. ⟦q30.6|17⟧ And he says to her, "What is the matter with thee?" ⟦q30.6|18⟧ She said, "I long to become a Christian, and no one will speak for me." ⟦q30.6|19⟧ He says to her, "Dost thou long for it in truth?" ⟦q30.6|20⟧ She said, "Yes, I beg thee." ⟦q30.6|21⟧ He says to her, "Do not lose heart at all. ⟦q30.6|22⟧ I am bringing some people, and they will take thee to the church." ⟦q30.6|23⟧ So he brings two others, angels too, and they carry her off to the church. ⟦q30.6|24⟧ And again they change their form into certain distinguished persons of the staff of the Augustal; and they call the clerics appointed over the baptisms. ⟦q30.6|25⟧ And the clerics say to them, "Does your charity stand surety for her?" ⟦q30.6|26⟧ They say to them, "Yes." ⟦q30.6|27⟧ So the clerics, having done what the order of service requires for those about to be baptized, baptized her in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit. ⟦q30.6|28⟧ And they put on her the garments of the newly baptized as well; and wearing white she went in, carried by them; and having set her down, they vanished.

⟦q30.6|29⟧ So her neighbours, seeing her wearing white, said to her, "Who baptized thee?" ⟦q30.6|30⟧ And she told them, saying, "Some people came and took me to the church, and spoke to the clerics, and they baptized me." ⟦q30.6|31⟧ And they said to her, "Who are these people?" ⟦q30.6|32⟧ So since she could not tell them, they went away and reported it to the pope. ⟦q30.6|33⟧ So the pope calls those over the baptistery. ⟦q30.6|34⟧ And he says to them, "Did you baptize this woman?" ⟦q30.6|35⟧ So they confessed that they had, at the request of So-and-so and So-and-so of the Augustal's staff. ⟦q30.6|36⟧ So the bishop sends for those who were named, and asks them whether they had stood surety for her. ⟦q30.6|37⟧ And they said, "We neither know nor are acquainted with those who did this." ⟦q30.6|38⟧ And then the bishop knew that the thing was of God. ⟦q30.6|39⟧ And calling her to him, he said, "Tell me, daughter, what good hast thou done?" ⟦q30.6|40⟧ She says to him, "Being a harlot and poor, what good had I to do?" ⟦q30.6|41⟧ He says to her, "Dost thou know of no good thing at all that thou hast done?" ⟦q30.6|42⟧ She says to him, "No; except that I saw a man wanting to hang himself, choked by his creditor, and I gave him all my substance and set him free." ⟦q30.6|43⟧ And having said this she went to her rest in the Lord, set free herself from her offences, willing and unwilling. ⟦q30.6|44⟧ Then the bishop glorified the Lord and said, "Righteous art thou, O Lord, and upright are thy judgements."

⟦q31.1|1+2⟧ CHAPTER 208

⟦q31.1|1+2⟧ A brother held fast by grief asked an elder, saying, "What shall I do? ⟦q31.1|1+2⟧ For my thoughts set upon me, saying, 'Thou didst renounce out of season; thou canst not be saved.'" The elder says to him, "Dost thou know, brother, that even if we cannot enter into the land of the promise, it is better for our carcasses to fall in the desert than to turn back to Egypt?"

⟦q31.2|1⟧ [3101] CHAPTER 209

⟦q31.2|1⟧ [bid:c3101a]One of the saints said: "When we pray to the Lord saying, 'Lead us not into temptation,' we do not say it so that we may not be tempted, for that is impossible; but so that we may not be swallowed up by the temptation by doing something displeasing to God. ⟦q31.2|2⟧ For this is what it is not to enter into temptation. ⟦q31.2|2⟧ For the holy martyrs too, tempted by tortures and not overcome, did not enter into temptation; just as neither does the man who fights with a wild beast, so long as he is not eaten by it; but when he is eaten, then he has entered into the temptation. ⟦q31.2|3⟧ So too with every passion, so long as one is not overcome by the passion."

⟦q31.3|1⟧ CHAPTER 210

⟦q31.3|1⟧ One of the Fathers told us that two bishops, near neighbours of each other, once fell into coolness toward each other. ⟦q31.3|2⟧ One of them was rich, the other humbler; and the rich one sought to do the other harm. ⟦q31.3|3⟧ The other heard of it, and knowing what he was going to do, he said to his clergy, "We shall win, by the grace of Christ." ⟦q31.3|4⟧ And they said to him, "Who can stand against that man, my lord?" ⟦q31.3|5⟧ He said to them, "Wait, and you will see." ⟦q31.3|6⟧ So he watches his time; and when his fellow bishop was holding a feast of holy martyrs, he takes his clergy and says to them, "Follow me, and we shall win." ⟦q31.3|7⟧ They said among themselves, "What can he be going to do?" ⟦q31.3|8⟧ And he comes to him. ⟦q31.3|9⟧ And as the other was coming with the procession, as they drew near, he falls at his feet with his clergy, saying, "Forgive us, my lord; we are thy servants." ⟦q31.3|10⟧ And the other, astounded at what he had done and pierced in his soul, God having changed his heart, takes hold of his feet, saying, "Thou art my lord and father." ⟦q31.3|10⟧ And from then on a great love arose between them. ⟦q31.3|11⟧ And he said to his clergy, "Did I not tell you that we should win by the grace of God? ⟦q31.3|12⟧ So you too, when you have anything against one another, do this, and you will win."

⟦q31.3|13⟧ The elder said again: "The humble man outdoes even the king himself in glory; for the one is praised to his face only, but the humble man is praised and called blessed both to his face and behind it."

⟦q31.3|13⟧ CHAPTER 211

⟦q31.4|1⟧ One of the superiors told us: "There lived near our coenobium a certain elder; and he was good in the things of the soul. ⟦q31.4|2⟧ And a brother lived next to him. ⟦q31.4|3⟧ And while the elder was away, the brother, being worked upon, opened [3104] his cell, went in and took his books and his belongings. ⟦q31.4|4⟧ So when the elder came and opened the cell and found his things carried off, he went to the brother to tell him; and he found the things still lying out in the middle, for the brother had not yet put them away. ⟦q31.4|5⟧ And the elder, not wanting to shame him or to convict him, made as though his stomach were troubling him, and went out and stayed a good while as though at the call of nature, until the things could be put away out of the middle. ⟦q31.4|6⟧ And coming back after this, the elder began to ask him about some other matter, and did not convict the brother.

⟦q31.4|7+8+9+10+11+12⟧ "A few days later the elder's things were found, and some people took the brother and put him in prison, though the elder had learned nothing at all of it. ⟦q31.4|7+8+9+10+11+12⟧ But when he heard about the brother, that he was in prison, not knowing the cause for which he was in prison, he came," says the superior, "to me (for he often visited us), and he says to me, 'Do me a charity; give me a few eggs and a few white loaves.' I say to him, 'Surely thou hast some guests today?' He says to me, 'Yes.' And the elder took them [?] to the prison, to comfort the brother. ⟦q31.4|7+8+9+10+11+12⟧ So when he went into the prison, the brother falls at his feet and says to him, 'It is because of thee that I am here, abba; for I am the one who stole thy things. ⟦q31.4|7+8+9+10+11+12⟧ But see, thy book is with So-and-so, and thy cloak with So-and-so.' The elder says to him, 'Let thy heart be assured, child: it was not for this that I came in. ⟦q31.4|13⟧ For I did not know at all that thou wast here on my account; but hearing that thou wast here, I was grieved, and I came in to comfort thee; and see, here are the white loaves and the eggs. ⟦q31.4|14+15⟧ But I will do everything until I get thee out of the prison.' And he went away and appealed to some of the great men, for he was known to them for his virtue; and they sent and had him brought out of the prison."

⟦q31.5|1+2+3+4+5⟧ CHAPTER 212

⟦q31.5|1+2+3+4+5⟧ One of the elders used to say: "A virtuous elder visited us; and we were reading in the garden the sayings of the holy Fathers. ⟦q31.5|1+2+3+4+5⟧ For the elder always loved to go through them, and he breathed them in within himself, which is why he had reaped every virtue from them. ⟦q31.5|1+2+3+4+5⟧ And when we came to that elder to whom the robbers came and said to him, 'We have come to take everything in thy cell'; and he said, 'Take whatever seems good to you, children.' They took everything and went off, and forgot the purse that was hanging in the [cell] [?]. ⟦q31.5|1+2+3+4+5⟧ So the elder," he says, "took it and ran after them, shouting and saying to them, 'Children, take from me what you forgot in [3105] [bid:c3105a]our cell.' And they, marvelling at the elder's forbearance, restored to him everything in his cell, and repented, saying to one another, 'Truly this is a man of God.'

⟦q31.5|1+2+3+4+5⟧ "So when we had read this, the elder says to me, 'Dost thou know, abba? ⟦q31.5|6+7⟧ This saying profited me greatly.' I say to him, 'How, father?' And he says to me, 'Once, when I was in the places along the Jordan, I read this, and I marvelled at the elder, and I said, "Lord, who hast counted me worthy to come to this habit, count me worthy to walk in this man's footsteps." ⟦q31.5|8⟧ So while I had this longing, two days later, see, robbers fall upon me. ⟦q31.5|9⟧ So when they knocked at the door and I knew they were robbers, I said to myself, "Thanks be to God; see, here is a time to show the fruit of my longing." ⟦q31.5|10⟧ I opened and received them with cheerfulness, and I lit a lamp and began to show them the things, saying, "Do not be alarmed; I trust in the Lord that I will not hide anything from you." ⟦q31.5|11⟧ They say to me, "Hast thou gold?" ⟦q31.5|12⟧ I say to them, "Yes, I have three gold pieces." ⟦q31.5|13⟧ So I opened the box in front of them. ⟦q31.5|14+15⟧ And they took it and went off in peace.' And I said to him playfully, 'Did they come back, like those men to the elder?' And he says to me at once, 'No; God forbid! ⟦q31.5|16⟧ For that was not what I wanted, that they should come back.'"

⟦q31.5|17⟧ CHAPTER 213

⟦q31.6|1+2⟧ One of the elders used to speak of divine wonders that take place even now in the Church of God, because of the godless heresies that have sprung up and are springing up, and above all because of the schisms of Severus the Acephalite and of the other destroyers: for the security and confirmation of the weaker souls, and for the conversion of those very men, if they would have it. ⟦q31.6|1+2⟧ For besides [?] the signs that come about daily through the divine Fathers, and from of old through the holy martyrs, in the catholic Church of God, [there are these] even today.

⟦q31.6|1+2⟧ CHAPTER 214

⟦q31.7|1⟧ Soruda is a village in the territory of the city of the Coaneni, in which there is a font which on the holy Theophany sweats, and from the sweat is filled over three hours; and after the baptism, again, not all at once but little by little, over the same three hours, it goes down.

⟦q31.8|1⟧ [3108] CHAPTER 215

⟦q31.8|1⟧ And there is also in the village of Kedrebata, which is in the territory of the city of the Oenoandans, a font which at the paschal feast of the Resurrection, though it is of a single stone, fills of itself, suddenly, and keeps its water until Pentecost; and suddenly after Pentecost the water disappears. ⟦q31.8|2⟧ Both these wonders are in the province of Lycia. ⟦q31.8|3⟧ If anyone will not believe them, it is no great trouble to go as far as Lycia, for full assurance of the truth.

⟦q31.8|4⟧ CHAPTER 216

⟦q31.8|4⟧ Another time, when I was in the holy city, a certain Christ-loving man comes and says to me, "Since we have had a little unpleasantness with each other, I and my brother, and he will not be reconciled to me, do me a kindness and speak to him." ⟦q31.8|5⟧ I received the word gladly, and calling his brother, I spoke to him the things that lead to love and peace. ⟦q31.8|6⟧ And he seemed to be persuaded by me. ⟦q31.8|7⟧ But afterwards he says to me, "I cannot be reconciled to him, for I swore by the cross." ⟦q31.8|8+9⟧ And I smiled and said to him, "Thy oath has this force: 'By the precious cross, O Christ, I will not keep thy commandments, but I will do the will of thine enemy the devil.' For we ought not only not to stand by what was wrongly resolved by us, but rather to repent and grieve over the things we wrongly resolved against ourselves, as the God-bearing Basil says. ⟦q31.8|10⟧ For Herod too, if he had repented and not stood by his oath, would not have wrought that great sin of beheading the forerunner of Christ." ⟦q31.8|11⟧ And besides, I brought him the text of holy Basil, which he brought from the Gospel, when the Lord wished to wash the feet of holy Peter and he refused.

⟦q31.8|11⟧ CHAPTER 217

⟦q31.9|1⟧ An elder said: "Little children, salt is from water, and if it comes near water, it dissolves and vanishes. ⟦q31.9|2⟧ So too the monk is from woman, and if he comes near a woman, he dissolves, and comes to his end: to not being, that is, a monk."

⟦q31.10|1⟧ CHAPTER 218

⟦q31.10|1⟧ Abba Sergius, the superior of the monastery of abba Constantine, told us: "Once, when we were travelling with a certain holy elder, we lost our way; and without our wishing it, and without our knowing where we were going, we found ourselves in the sown fields, and we [3109] [bid:c3109a]trampled a little of the crop. ⟦q31.10|2⟧ And the farmer, who happened to be working there, noticed it and began to abuse us a great deal in anger, and to say: 'You monks? ⟦q31.10|3+4+5+6+7⟧ You fear God? ⟦q31.10|3+4+5+6+7⟧ If you had the fear of God before your eyes, you would not have done this.' At once the holy elder says to us, 'For the Lord's sake, let no one speak.' And he said to him, 'Thou sayest well, my child; for if we had the fear of God, we should not have done these things.' Again the man abused us in a rage. ⟦q31.10|3+4+5+6+7⟧ Again the elder says, 'Thou speakest in truth, child: if we were true monks, we would not have done this. ⟦q31.10|8+9⟧ But for the Lord's sake, forgive, for we have sinned.' So he, astounded, came and threw himself at the elder's feet, saying, 'I have sinned; forgive me. ⟦q31.10|10+11⟧ And for the Lord's sake take me with you.'" And the blessed Sergius said that he did in truth follow with them, and came and received the habit.

⟦q31.10|10+11⟧ CHAPTER 219

⟦q31.11|1⟧ An elder told me such a thing as this: "Once I stayed a short time in the laura of abba Gerasimus, and I had a certain friend there. ⟦q31.11|2⟧ One day as we were sitting and talking about what profits the soul, I remembered this saying of abba Poemen. ⟦q31.11|3+4+5⟧ And he says to me, 'I, father, have experience of these words, and of the rest their working brings. ⟦q31.11|3+4+5⟧ For once I had a certain deacon [?] of the laura, a true friend, and I do not know from where, he came to have a suspicion of me about a matter that brought him grief, and he began to be gloomy with me. ⟦q31.11|3+4+5⟧ And seeing him gloomy, I asked to learn the cause. ⟦q31.11|6⟧ And he says to me, "Thou didst such-and-such a thing." ⟦q31.11|7⟧ But I, not conscious of having done any such thing at all, began to assure him: "I am not conscious of having done this." ⟦q31.11|8⟧ He says to me, "Forgive; I am not assured." ⟦q31.11|9⟧ So I withdrew to my cell and began to search my heart, whether any such thing had been done by me, and I did not find it. ⟦q31.11|10⟧ Then I see him holding the holy cup and giving Communion; and I swear to him by it that I am not conscious of having done this. ⟦q31.11|10⟧ And he was not persuaded. ⟦q31.11|11⟧ So coming again to myself, and remembering these words of the holy Fathers, and believing them, I turned my thought a little, and I say within myself, "The deacon loves me truly, and moved by love he has made bold to tell me what his heart held about me, so that I may be sober and keep watch on myself from now on and not do it. ⟦q31.11|12⟧ But, wretched soul, since thou sayest, 'I did not do this thing,' [3112] [bid:c3112a]have not countless evils been done by thee, and they have slipped from thee? ⟦q31.11|13⟧ Where are the things thou didst yesterday or the day before, or ten days ago? ⟦q31.11|13⟧ Dost thou remember them? ⟦q31.11|14⟧ So then, this too thou hast done, like those, and it has slipped from thee, as the earlier ones did." ⟦q31.11|15⟧ And so I set my heart to it that in truth I had done it, but that as the earlier things had slipped from me, so had this. ⟦q31.11|16⟧ So I began to give thanks to God, and to the deacon, because through him the Lord had counted me worthy to recognize my fault; and I repented of it. ⟦q31.11|17+18⟧ So I rose up with such thoughts and went to make a prostration to the deacon and to thank him, because through him I had come to know my fault. ⟦q31.11|17+18⟧ And as I knocked at his door, he opened, and makes a prostration to me first, saying, "Forgive me, for I was mocked by demons, suspecting thee in that matter. ⟦q31.11|19+20+21⟧ For in truth God has assured me that thou hast nothing to do with the matter." ⟦q31.11|19+20+21⟧ And,' he said, 'he would not even let me assure him, saying, "There is no need."'" And being greatly profited, I glorified the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit, to whom belong might and majesty unto the ages of ages. ⟦q31.11|19+20+21⟧ Amen.
